But it’s not a clear thought. It’s just this strong feeling that I shouldn’t be there, and then I find an excuse to stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something specific might happen if you are there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say it’s something specific. That’s the hard part to explain. It’s not like I have one clear thought, like, “If I stand by the front windows, this exact thing is going to happen.” I wish it were that simple, honestly, because then maybe I could argue with it or talk myself down.\n\nWhat I do know is that I can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore. I came in because it’s become this whole thing at work where I keep avoiding it, and I know I’m avoiding it. I’ll stay in the back and tell myself I’m checking stock, or I’ll start reorganizing something that doesn’t really need to be reorganized, or I’ll wait and hope someone else handles the front. And the whole time I know that being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo… no, not specific exactly. More like the front of the store just feels unsafe or wrong, and I react like I need to stay away from it before I even have a clear reason in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of being near the front of the store related to something that happened to you in the past at that location?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it is connected to that place, to the front of the store specifically. I don’t know how else to explain it except that since what happened there, I just can’t make myself be near the front windows or even that same part of the street anymore.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. It’s not like I’m just being lazy or trying to get out of doing part of my job. I know being up front is part of the job, and I used to just do it. But now I keep finding reasons to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, taking longer than I need to back there — anything so I don’t have to stand near the windows or be by that front area.\n\nSo yes, it’s related to what happened there. I just don’t feel able to go back to that part of the store like normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened at the front of the store that's making it hard for you to go back there?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s mostly that front area itself, the windows and the street right outside. I know that sounds like I’m not answering the question directly, but that’s what’s stuck for me. Since what happened there, I just can’t make myself go back to that spot like I used to.\n\nI came in because it’s affecting my job now. I’m supposed to be up front with customers sometimes, near the windows, watching the register area, helping people when they come in. And instead I keep drifting to the back of the store. I’ll tell myself I’m checking inventory, or fixing shelves, or that something in the stockroom needs doing, but honestly a lot of the time I’m just trying not to be near the front.\n\nIt’s not even always a full decision, like, “I am avoiding this.” Sometimes I just realize I’ve made three little excuses in a row to stay away from that part of the store. And I know it’s because of what happened there, because before that I didn’t think twice about standing there or looking out those windows. Now that whole front area feels like somewhere I shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happened at the front of the store that's causing your fear?\n\nPatient: It was a crash. That’s the part I keep seeing, even when I’m not trying to think about it. I’ll be at work trying to do something normal, like set up a display or straighten items near the front, and then out of nowhere it’s like my brain just throws me right back into it.\n\nI can hear the bang again — really loud, like it’s happening right then — and I can see the glass coming down. That’s the image that keeps popping in, the glass coming down from the front windows. It’s not like I’m sitting there deciding to remember it or replay it. It just cuts in suddenly, and then I’m not really focused on the display or the shelf anymore. I’m just back at that front part of the store in my head, hearing it and seeing it.\n\nSo when you ask what happened, that’s what I mean. There was the crash at the front, and now the sound and the glass are kind of stuck with me. Even if I’m just trying to do my job, it comes back like it’s happening again, and that’s why being near that area feels so hard.\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific incident or accident at the front of the store that you think might be causing your fear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was. It was that crash at the front of the store. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s the thing my mind keeps going back to.\n\nWhat’s been scaring me isn’t just remembering that it happened, it’s how it keeps popping into my head when I’m trying to do normal work things. Like if I’m setting up a display near the front, or even thinking about going over there, suddenly I can hear the bang again, really sharp and loud, and I can see the glass coming down. It feels so immediate, like for a second I’m not just remembering it, I’m back in that moment.\n\nAnd it happens out of nowhere. I’ll be focused on arranging something or trying to help with the front area, and then it’s just there in my head before I can stop it. That’s why I think that incident is what’s causing the fear. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. It catches me off guard every time, because I’m not trying to think about it. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of brings it back all at once, and for a second it feels like I’m back in it instead of just being at work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash, such as the sound or the broken glass, that you think is causing your fear of being near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s definitely those parts. The sound and the glass. I know I keep coming back to that, but that’s what happens in my head. And it’s embarrassing because being near the front and dealing with customers is part of my job, so I can’t exactly avoid it forever, but lately I keep trying to anyway.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the front of the store that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s the front windows, mainly, and that stretch of street right outside them. I don’t know how to explain it without sounding repetitive, but that’s really the part I can’t handle.\n\nIt’s like once I’m supposed to go up there, my mind immediately starts looking for a way not to. I’ll suddenly decide I should go to the back and check stock, or that something needs organizing, or I’ll kind of linger where the boxes are because it feels easier to justify being there. And the whole time I know what I’m doing. I know I’m avoiding the front. It’s not like I’m accidentally busy.\n\nThat’s basically why I came in. I can’t make myself go near those windows or that part of the street anymore, even though being up front with customers is literally part of my job. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back, and I’m embarrassed by it, but I still do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen to you if you went near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I believe something specific would happen, like I don’t have a clear picture of someone coming in or something happening right then. It’s more… I just can’t get myself to go over there anymore.\n\nThe front windows and that part of the street feel like this place I’m not supposed to be near, even though I know that sounds strange. And it’s gotten to where I’m constantly finding reasons to stay in the back. Like I’ll say I need to check stock, or organize something, or I’ll wait for someone else to handle the front, even though I know being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess it’s not exactly, “If I go there, this bad thing will happen.” It’s more that my body acts like something bad could happen, and I end up avoiding it before I even really think it through. That’s a big reason I came in, because I can’t keep doing my job like this, but I also can’t seem to make myself go near that part of the store.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something would happen to you personally if you were near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I think, like, something is specifically going to happen to me personally. I don’t have a clear thought like, “If I stand there, this exact thing will happen to me.” It’s more that I just can’t make myself be there.\n\nLike, near the front windows or that part of the street, I immediately feel like I need to get away from it, or not go there in the first place. And I know I’ve kind of said this already, but that’s really the reason I came in. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, doing basically anything that keeps me away from the front — even though being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess personally, it feels like something could happen to me, but it’s not a thought I can explain clearly. It’s more that my whole reaction is to avoid that area, and I can’t seem to stop doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something might happen to you or someone else if you are there?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in the sense that it feels like that, but I don’t have a neat, specific fear I can put into words. It’s not like I’m standing there thinking, “If I go to the front, this exact thing will happen to me,” or “someone else is definitely in danger.” It’s more that the front windows and that part of the street feel wrong now, like my body reacts before I can reason with it.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I can’t make myself go near that area anymore. I’ll tell myself I’m just checking something in the back, or that the stockroom needs organizing, or I’ll wait for someone else to deal with whoever’s up front. And I know I’m doing it. I know it’s avoidance, because being at the front with customers is part of my job, and I’m still choosing anything that keeps me away from those windows.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I’m afraid something might happen, I guess yes, kind of. But it’s not a clear thought. It’s just this strong feeling that I shouldn’t be there, and then I find an excuse to stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something specific might happen if you are there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say it’s something specific. That’s the hard part to explain. It’s not like I have one clear thought, like, “If I stand by the front windows, this exact thing is going to happen.” I wish it were that simple, honestly, because then maybe I could argue with it or talk myself down.\n\nWhat I do know is that I can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore. I came in because it’s become this whole thing at work where I keep avoiding it, and I know I’m avoiding it. I’ll stay in the back and tell myself I’m checking stock, or I’ll start reorganizing something that doesn’t really need to be reorganized, or I’ll wait and hope someone else handles the front. And the whole time I know that being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo… no, not specific exactly. More like the front of the store just feels unsafe or wrong, and I react like I need to stay away from it before I even have a clear reason in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of being near the front of the store related to something that happened to you in the past at that location?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it is connected to that place, to the front of the store specifically. I don’t know how else to explain it except that since what happened there, I just can’t make myself be near the front windows or even that same part of the street anymore.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. It’s not like I’m just being lazy or trying to get out of doing part of my job. I know being up front is part of the job, and I used to just do it. But now I keep finding reasons to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, taking longer than I need to back there — anything so I don’t have to stand near the windows or be by that front area.\n\nSo yes, it’s related to what happened there. I just don’t feel able to go back to that part of the store like normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened at the front of the store that's making it hard for you to go back there?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s mostly that front area itself, the windows and the street right outside. I know that sounds like I’m not answering the question directly, but that’s what’s stuck for me. Since what happened there, I just can’t make myself go back to that spot like I used to.\n\nI came in because it’s affecting my job now. I’m supposed to be up front with customers sometimes, near the windows, watching the register area, helping people when they come in. And instead I keep drifting to the back of the store. I’ll tell myself I’m checking inventory, or fixing shelves, or that something in the stockroom needs doing, but honestly a lot of the time I’m just trying not to be near the front.\n\nIt’s not even always a full decision, like, “I am avoiding this.” Sometimes I just realize I’ve made three little excuses in a row to stay away from that part of the store. And I know it’s because of what happened there, because before that I didn’t think twice about standing there or looking out those windows. Now that whole front area feels like somewhere I shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happened at the front of the store that's causing your fear?\n\nPatient: It was a crash. That’s the part I keep seeing, even when I’m not trying to think about it. I’ll be at work trying to do something normal, like set up a display or straighten items near the front, and then out of nowhere it’s like my brain just throws me right back into it.\n\nI can hear the bang again — really loud, like it’s happening right then — and I can see the glass coming down. That’s the image that keeps popping in, the glass coming down from the front windows. It’s not like I’m sitting there deciding to remember it or replay it. It just cuts in suddenly, and then I’m not really focused on the display or the shelf anymore. I’m just back at that front part of the store in my head, hearing it and seeing it.\n\nSo when you ask what happened, that’s what I mean. There was the crash at the front, and now the sound and the glass are kind of stuck with me. Even if I’m just trying to do my job, it comes back like it’s happening again, and that’s why being near that area feels so hard.\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific incident or accident at the front of the store that you think might be causing your fear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was. It was that crash at the front of the store. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s the thing my mind keeps going back to.\n\nWhat’s been scaring me isn’t just remembering that it happened, it’s how it keeps popping into my head when I’m trying to do normal work things. Like if I’m setting up a display near the front, or even thinking about going over there, suddenly I can hear the bang again, really sharp and loud, and I can see the glass coming down. It feels so immediate, like for a second I’m not just remembering it, I’m back in that moment.\n\nAnd it happens out of nowhere. I’ll be focused on arranging something or trying to help with the front area, and then it’s just there in my head before I can stop it. That’s why I think that incident is what’s causing the fear. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of sets it off, and then my mind goes right back to that moment.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, it was at the store where I work, so in that sense it involved me because I was there and it happened in the place I’m supposed to feel familiar with. Even just seeing the front area from inside the store makes me want to turn around and disappear into the back.\n\nDoctor: Is there something outside the store, like people or objects, that you're afraid of when you're near the front windows?\n\nPatient: Not exactly people or objects, no. I mean, I guess there are people out there sometimes, customers walking by, cars, whatever, but it’s not like I’m scared of a specific person standing there or one particular thing on the sidewalk.\n\nIt’s more that the whole front area feels like the problem now — the windows, being able to see out onto that part of the street, being close to it. I came in because I just can’t make myself go near there anymore, and I keep making up reasons to stay in the back. Like I’ll suddenly need to check stock, or I’ll say I’m sorting boxes, or I’ll take any task that keeps me away from the front, even though being out there is part of my job. So it’s not really one object or one person outside. It’s just that area.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this way about being near front windows or exposed areas in other locations, or is it just this particular store?\n\nPatient: It’s mostly that store. I mean, I’ve noticed I don’t love being near big windows in general right now, but the thing I came in about is really the front of my store — those windows and that exact part of the street outside.\n\nThat’s where I just… I can’t make myself go over there. I keep finding ways not to. Like I’ll suddenly “need” to go check stock, or I’ll stay in the back sorting boxes, or I’ll make it seem like I’m doing something useful when really I’m just trying not to be near the front. And it’s frustrating because that’s literally part of my job, being up there with customers and near the windows, but I keep avoiding it anyway. It’s not like every exposed place everywhere feels exactly the same. It’s that particular front area that feels impossible right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you avoid the front of the store because you're afraid of having a panic attack or feeling overwhelmed when you're there?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s exactly that I’m afraid of having a panic attack, like that’s not the thought in my head. It’s more that I just can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore.\n\nLike, the second I know I might have to be up there, I start figuring out ways to avoid it. I’ll tell myself I should go check stock, or that the back room needs organizing, or that maybe someone else can handle the register for a bit. And I know that sounds like I’m just being difficult or lazy, but I’m not. I used to do that part of the job all the time without even thinking about it.\n\nNow it’s like my whole body decides, no, don’t go there. Stay in the back. Find a reason. So yes, I guess I’m avoiding it because I feel like I’ll get overwhelmed being there, but it’s not really about being scared of the feeling itself. It’s more that being near the front feels impossible right now, and I keep making excuses so I don’t have to face it.\n\nDoctor: Is there something that you think might happen to you if you were to go near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I have one clear thing in my head, like, “this exact thing is going to happen.” It’s not that specific. It’s more like if I go near the front windows, or that part of the street, my brain just treats it like I shouldn’t be there.\n\nThat’s kind of why I came in, honestly. I can’t make myself go over there anymore. Even before I’m actually near it, I start looking for ways out of it. I’ll think, okay, I need to check something in the stockroom, or I should go sort boxes, or maybe I can ask someone else to cover the front for a minute. And the whole time I know I’m doing it. I know I’m making excuses. But I still do it.\n\nSo it’s not like I’m picturing one definite thing happening to me. It’s more that being up there feels like something bad could happen, or like I just really need to get away from that area. And it’s embarrassing because being near the front and dealing with customers is part of my job, so I can’t exactly avoid it forever, but lately I keep trying to anyway.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the front of the store that you're trying to avoid?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s the front windows, mainly, and that stretch of street right outside them. I don’t know how to explain it without sounding repetitive, but that’s really the part I can’t handle.\n\nIt’s like once I’m supposed to go up there, my mind immediately starts looking for a way not to. I’ll suddenly decide I should go to the back and check stock, or that something needs organizing, or I’ll kind of linger where the boxes are because it feels easier to justify being there. And the whole time I know what I’m doing. I know I’m avoiding the front. It’s not like I’m accidentally busy.\n\nThat’s basically why I came in. I can’t make myself go near those windows or that part of the street anymore, even though being up front with customers is literally part of my job. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back, and I’m embarrassed by it, but I still do it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something bad would happen to you if you went near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I believe something specific would happen, like I don’t have a clear picture of someone coming in or something happening right then. It’s more… I just can’t get myself to go over there anymore.\n\nThe front windows and that part of the street feel like this place I’m not supposed to be near, even though I know that sounds strange. And it’s gotten to where I’m constantly finding reasons to stay in the back. Like I’ll say I need to check stock, or organize something, or I’ll wait for someone else to handle the front, even though I know being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess it’s not exactly, “If I go there, this bad thing will happen.” It’s more that my body acts like something bad could happen, and I end up avoiding it before I even really think it through. That’s a big reason I came in, because I can’t keep doing my job like this, but I also can’t seem to make myself go near that part of the store.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something would happen to you personally if you were near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I think, like, something is specifically going to happen to me personally. I don’t have a clear thought like, “If I stand there, this exact thing will happen to me.” It’s more that I just can’t make myself be there.\n\nLike, near the front windows or that part of the street, I immediately feel like I need to get away from it, or not go there in the first place. And I know I’ve kind of said this already, but that’s really the reason I came in. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, doing basically anything that keeps me away from the front — even though being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess personally, it feels like something could happen to me, but it’s not a thought I can explain clearly. It’s more that my whole reaction is to avoid that area, and I can’t seem to stop doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something might happen to you or someone else if you are there?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in the sense that it feels like that, but I don’t have a neat, specific fear I can put into words. It’s not like I’m standing there thinking, “If I go to the front, this exact thing will happen to me,” or “someone else is definitely in danger.” It’s more that the front windows and that part of the street feel wrong now, like my body reacts before I can reason with it.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I can’t make myself go near that area anymore. I’ll tell myself I’m just checking something in the back, or that the stockroom needs organizing, or I’ll wait for someone else to deal with whoever’s up front. And I know I’m doing it. I know it’s avoidance, because being at the front with customers is part of my job, and I’m still choosing anything that keeps me away from those windows.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I’m afraid something might happen, I guess yes, kind of. But it’s not a clear thought. It’s just this strong feeling that I shouldn’t be there, and then I find an excuse to stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something specific might happen if you are there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say it’s something specific. That’s the hard part to explain. It’s not like I have one clear thought, like, “If I stand by the front windows, this exact thing is going to happen.” I wish it were that simple, honestly, because then maybe I could argue with it or talk myself down.\n\nWhat I do know is that I can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore. I came in because it’s become this whole thing at work where I keep avoiding it, and I know I’m avoiding it. I’ll stay in the back and tell myself I’m checking stock, or I’ll start reorganizing something that doesn’t really need to be reorganized, or I’ll wait and hope someone else handles the front. And the whole time I know that being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo… no, not specific exactly. More like the front of the store just feels unsafe or wrong, and I react like I need to stay away from it before I even have a clear reason in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of being near the front of the store related to something that happened to you in the past at that location?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it is connected to that place, to the front of the store specifically. I don’t know how else to explain it except that since what happened there, I just can’t make myself be near the front windows or even that same part of the street anymore.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. It’s not like I’m just being lazy or trying to get out of doing part of my job. I know being up front is part of the job, and I used to just do it. But now I keep finding reasons to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, taking longer than I need to back there — anything so I don’t have to stand near the windows or be by that front area.\n\nSo yes, it’s related to what happened there. I just don’t feel able to go back to that part of the store like normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened at the front of the store that's making it hard for you to go back there?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s mostly that front area itself, the windows and the street right outside. I know that sounds like I’m not answering the question directly, but that’s what’s stuck for me. Since what happened there, I just can’t make myself go back to that spot like I used to.\n\nI came in because it’s affecting my job now. I’m supposed to be up front with customers sometimes, near the windows, watching the register area, helping people when they come in. And instead I keep drifting to the back of the store. I’ll tell myself I’m checking inventory, or fixing shelves, or that something in the stockroom needs doing, but honestly a lot of the time I’m just trying not to be near the front.\n\nIt’s not even always a full decision, like, “I am avoiding this.” Sometimes I just realize I’ve made three little excuses in a row to stay away from that part of the store. And I know it’s because of what happened there, because before that I didn’t think twice about standing there or looking out those windows. Now that whole front area feels like somewhere I shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happened at the front of the store that's causing your fear?\n\nPatient: It was a crash. That’s the part I keep seeing, even when I’m not trying to think about it. I’ll be at work trying to do something normal, like set up a display or straighten items near the front, and then out of nowhere it’s like my brain just throws me right back into it.\n\nI can hear the bang again — really loud, like it’s happening right then — and I can see the glass coming down. That’s the image that keeps popping in, the glass coming down from the front windows. It’s not like I’m sitting there deciding to remember it or replay it. It just cuts in suddenly, and then I’m not really focused on the display or the shelf anymore. I’m just back at that front part of the store in my head, hearing it and seeing it.\n\nSo when you ask what happened, that’s what I mean. There was the crash at the front, and now the sound and the glass are kind of stuck with me. Even if I’m just trying to do my job, it comes back like it’s happening again, and that’s why being near that area feels so hard.\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific incident or accident at the front of the store that you think might be causing your fear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was. It was that crash at the front of the store. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s the thing my mind keeps going back to.\n\nWhat’s been scaring me isn’t just remembering that it happened, it’s how it keeps popping into my head when I’m trying to do normal work things. Like if I’m setting up a display near the front, or even thinking about going over there, suddenly I can hear the bang again, really sharp and loud, and I can see the glass coming down. It feels so immediate, like for a second I’m not just remembering it, I’m back in that moment.\n\nAnd it happens out of nowhere. I’ll be focused on arranging something or trying to help with the front area, and then it’s just there in my head before I can stop it. That’s why I think that incident is what’s causing the fear. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. It catches me off guard every time, because I’m not trying to think about it. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of brings it back all at once, and for a second it feels like I’m back in it instead of just being at work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash, such as the sound or the broken glass, that you think is causing your fear of being near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s definitely those parts. The sound and the glass. I know I keep coming back to that, but that’s what happens in my head. It’s like I can be doing something totally normal, like setting up a display near the front, trying to line things up or make it look nice, and then all of a sudden the crash just pops in.\n\nIt’s not like I decide to think about it. It just comes in out of nowhere, and suddenly I can hear that bang again, like really loud and sharp, and I see the glass coming down from the front windows. For a second it feels like it’s happening again, not like I’m just remembering it from before.\n\nSo, yes, I think the sound and the broken glass are the specific things that make being near the front so hard. The front area kind of sets it off, and then my mind goes right back to that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did the crash at the front of the store involve you directly or someone you know?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, it was at the store where I work, so in that sense it involved me because I was there and it happened in the place I’m supposed to feel familiar with. But if you mean was I the one hit, or was there someone close to me personally involved… I don’t think I can really say that.\n\nWhat I do know is that it felt close enough that I can’t separate myself from it, if that makes sense. It wasn’t just something I heard about afterward. It happened right there at the front of the store, and that’s the part that stuck with me. But as far as whether it was directly me or someone I know specifically, I’m not sure there’s more I can give you than that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I just don’t feel able to go back to that part of the store like normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened at the front of the store that's making it hard for you to go back there?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s mostly that front area itself, the windows and the street right outside. I know that sounds like I’m not answering the question directly, but that’s what’s stuck for me. Since what happened there, I just can’t make myself go back to that spot like I used to.\n\nI came in because it’s affecting my job now. I’m supposed to be up front with customers sometimes, near the windows, watching the register area, helping people when they come in. And instead I keep drifting to the back of the store. I’ll tell myself I’m checking inventory, or fixing shelves, or that something in the stockroom needs doing, but honestly a lot of the time I’m just trying not to be near the front.\n\nIt’s not even always a full decision, like, “I am avoiding this.” Sometimes I just realize I’ve made three little excuses in a row to stay away from that part of the store. And I know it’s because of what happened there, because before that I didn’t think twice about standing there or looking out those windows. Now that whole front area feels like somewhere I shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happened at the front of the store that's causing your fear?\n\nPatient: It was a crash. That’s the part I keep seeing, even when I’m not trying to think about it. I’ll be at work trying to do something normal, like set up a display or straighten items near the front, and then out of nowhere it’s like my brain just throws me right back into it.\n\nI can hear the bang again — really loud, like it’s happening right then — and I can see the glass coming down. That’s the image that keeps popping in, the glass coming down from the front windows. It’s not like I’m sitting there deciding to remember it or replay it. It just cuts in suddenly, and then I’m not really focused on the display or the shelf anymore. I’m just back at that front part of the store in my head, hearing it and seeing it.\n\nSo when you ask what happened, that’s what I mean. There was the crash at the front, and now the sound and the glass are kind of stuck with me. Even if I’m just trying to do my job, it comes back like it’s happening again, and that’s why being near that area feels so hard.\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific incident or accident at the front of the store that you think might be causing your fear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was. It was that crash at the front of the store. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s the thing my mind keeps going back to.\n\nWhat’s been scaring me isn’t just remembering that it happened, it’s how it keeps popping into my head when I’m trying to do normal work things. Like if I’m setting up a display near the front, or even thinking about going over there, suddenly I can hear the bang again, really sharp and loud, and I can see the glass coming down. It feels so immediate, like for a second I’m not just remembering it, I’m back in that moment.\n\nAnd it happens out of nowhere. I’ll be focused on arranging something or trying to help with the front area, and then it’s just there in my head before I can stop it. That’s why I think that incident is what’s causing the fear. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. It catches me off guard every time, because I’m not trying to think about it. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of brings it back all at once, and for a second it feels like I’m back in it instead of just being at work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash, such as the sound or the broken glass, that you think is causing your fear of being near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s definitely those parts. The sound and the glass. I know I keep coming back to that, but that’s what happens in my head. It’s like I can be doing something totally normal, like setting up a display near the front, trying to line things up or make it look nice, and then all of a sudden the crash just pops in.\n\nIt’s not like I decide to think about it. It just comes in out of nowhere, and suddenly I can hear that bang again, like really loud and sharp, and I see the glass coming down from the front windows. For a second it feels like it’s happening again, not like I’m just remembering it from before.\n\nSo, yes, I think the sound and the broken glass are the specific things that make being near the front so hard. The front area kind of sets it off, and then my mind goes right back to that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did the crash at the front of the store involve you directly or someone you know?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, it was at the store where I work, so in that sense it involved me because I was there and it happened in the place I’m supposed to feel familiar with. It wasn’t just something I heard about afterward. It happened right there at the front of the store, and that’s the part that stuck with me. I can’t make myself go near those windows or that part of the street anymore, even though being up front with customers is literally part of my job. And it’s gotten to where I’m constantly finding reasons to stay in the back. Like I’ll say I need to check stock, or organize something, or I’ll wait for someone else to handle the front, even though I know being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess it’s not exactly, “If I go there, this bad thing will happen.” It’s more that my body acts like something bad could happen, and I end up avoiding it before I even really think it through. That’s a big reason I came in, because I can’t keep doing my job like this, but I also can’t seem to make myself go near that part of the store.\n\nDoctor: Do you think something would happen to you personally if you were near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I think, like, something is specifically going to happen to me personally. I don’t have a clear thought like, “If I stand there, this exact thing will happen to me.” It’s more that I just can’t make myself be there.\n\nLike, near the front windows or that part of the street, I immediately feel like I need to get away from it, or not go there in the first place. And I know I’ve kind of said this already, but that’s really the reason I came in. I keep finding excuses to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, doing basically anything that keeps me away from the front — even though being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo I guess personally, it feels like something could happen to me, but it’s not a thought I can explain clearly. It’s more that my whole reaction is to avoid that area, and I can’t seem to stop doing that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something might happen to you or someone else if you are there?\n\nPatient: I think… yes, in the sense that it feels like that, but I don’t have a neat, specific fear I can put into words. It’s not like I’m standing there thinking, “If I go to the front, this exact thing will happen to me,” or “someone else is definitely in danger.” It’s more that the front windows and that part of the street feel wrong now, like my body reacts before I can reason with it.\n\nThat’s really why I came in. I can’t make myself go near that area anymore. I’ll tell myself I’m just checking something in the back, or that the stockroom needs organizing, or I’ll wait for someone else to deal with whoever’s up front. And I know I’m doing it. I know it’s avoidance, because being at the front with customers is part of my job, and I’m still choosing anything that keeps me away from those windows.\n\nSo if you’re asking whether I’m afraid something might happen, I guess yes, kind of. But it’s not a clear thought. It’s just this strong feeling that I shouldn’t be there, and then I find an excuse to stay in the back.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your avoidance of the front of the store is because you fear something specific might happen if you are there?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I can say it’s something specific. That’s the hard part to explain. It’s not like I have one clear thought, like, “If I stand by the front windows, this exact thing is going to happen.” I wish it were that simple, honestly, because then maybe I could argue with it or talk myself down.\n\nWhat I do know is that I can’t make myself go near the front windows or that part of the street anymore. I came in because it’s become this whole thing at work where I keep avoiding it, and I know I’m avoiding it. I’ll stay in the back and tell myself I’m checking stock, or I’ll start reorganizing something that doesn’t really need to be reorganized, or I’ll wait and hope someone else handles the front. And the whole time I know that being up there is part of my job.\n\nSo… no, not specific exactly. More like the front of the store just feels unsafe or wrong, and I react like I need to stay away from it before I even have a clear reason in my head.\n\nDoctor: Is your fear of being near the front of the store related to something that happened to you in the past at that location?\n\nPatient: Yes… I mean, I think it is connected to that place, to the front of the store specifically. I don’t know how else to explain it except that since what happened there, I just can’t make myself be near the front windows or even that same part of the street anymore.\n\nThat’s really what made me come in. It’s not like I’m just being lazy or trying to get out of doing part of my job. I know being up front is part of the job, and I used to just do it. But now I keep finding reasons to stay in the back — checking stock, organizing things, taking longer than I need to back there — anything so I don’t have to stand near the windows or be by that front area.\n\nSo yes, it’s related to what happened there. I just don’t feel able to go back to that part of the store like normal.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me what happened at the front of the store that's making it hard for you to go back there?\n\nPatient: It’s… it’s mostly that front area itself, the windows and the street right outside. I know that sounds like I’m not answering the question directly, but that’s what’s stuck for me. Since what happened there, I just can’t make myself go back to that spot like I used to.\n\nI came in because it’s affecting my job now. I’m supposed to be up front with customers sometimes, near the windows, watching the register area, helping people when they come in. And instead I keep drifting to the back of the store. I’ll tell myself I’m checking inventory, or fixing shelves, or that something in the stockroom needs doing, but honestly a lot of the time I’m just trying not to be near the front.\n\nIt’s not even always a full decision, like, “I am avoiding this.” Sometimes I just realize I’ve made three little excuses in a row to stay away from that part of the store. And I know it’s because of what happened there, because before that I didn’t think twice about standing there or looking out those windows. Now that whole front area feels like somewhere I shouldn’t be.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happened at the front of the store that's causing your fear?\n\nPatient: It was a crash. That’s the part I keep seeing, even when I’m not trying to think about it. I’ll be at work trying to do something normal, like set up a display or straighten items near the front, and then out of nowhere it’s like my brain just throws me right back into it.\n\nI can hear the bang again — really loud, like it’s happening right then — and I can see the glass coming down. That’s the image that keeps popping in, the glass coming down from the front windows. It’s not like I’m sitting there deciding to remember it or replay it. It just cuts in suddenly, and then I’m not really focused on the display or the shelf anymore. I’m just back at that front part of the store in my head, hearing it and seeing it.\n\nSo when you ask what happened, that’s what I mean. There was the crash at the front, and now the sound and the glass are kind of stuck with me. Even if I’m just trying to do my job, it comes back like it’s happening again, and that’s why being near that area feels so hard.\n\nDoctor: Was there a specific incident or accident at the front of the store that you think might be causing your fear?\n\nPatient: Yes, there was. It was that crash at the front of the store. I know I’ve kind of said it already, but that’s the thing my mind keeps going back to.\n\nWhat’s been scaring me isn’t just remembering that it happened, it’s how it keeps popping into my head when I’m trying to do normal work things. Like if I’m setting up a display near the front, or even thinking about going over there, suddenly I can hear the bang again, really sharp and loud, and I can see the glass coming down. It feels so immediate, like for a second I’m not just remembering it, I’m back in that moment.\n\nAnd it happens out of nowhere. I’ll be focused on arranging something or trying to help with the front area, and then it’s just there in my head before I can stop it. That’s why I think that incident is what’s causing the fear. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. It catches me off guard every time, because I’m not trying to think about it. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of brings it back all at once, and for a second it feels like I’m back in it instead of just being at work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash, such as the sound or the broken glass, that you think is causing your fear of being near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s definitely those parts. The sound and the glass. I know I keep coming back to that, but that’s what happens in my head. It’s like I can be doing something totally normal, like setting up a display near the front, trying to line things up or make it look nice, and then all of a sudden the crash just pops in.\n\nIt’s not like I decide to think about it. It just comes in out of nowhere, and suddenly I can hear that bang again, like really loud and sharp, and I see the glass coming down from the front windows. For a second it feels like it’s happening again, not like I’m just remembering it from before.\n\nSo, yes, I think the sound and the broken glass are the specific things that make being near the front so hard. The front area kind of sets it off, and then my mind goes right back to that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did the crash at the front of the store involve you directly or someone you know?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, it was at the store where I work, so in that sense it involved me because I was there and it happened in the place I’m supposed to feel familiar with. But if you mean was I the one hit, or was there someone close to me personally involved… I don’t think I can really say that.\n\nWhat I do know is that it felt close enough that I can’t separate myself from it, if that makes sense. It wasn’t just something I heard about afterward. It happened right there at the front of the store, and that’s the part that stuck with me. But as far as whether it was directly me or someone I know specifically, I’m not sure there’s more I can give you than that.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience or witness the crash at the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to separate those, honestly. Like… I was there at the store, and it happened at the front, so it wasn’t something I just heard about later from someone else. But when you say “experience or witness,” I don’t know exactly what counts.\n\nI don’t want to say it the wrong way or make it sound different than it was. I know I was there, and I know the front of the store is what’s tied to it for me. But whether I fully witnessed every part of the crash or was directly in it… I’m not sure how to explain that clearly. It all feels kind of jumbled when I try to put it into a straight answer.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. The front of the store doesn’t just feel like the front of the store anymore — it feels tied to that crash.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash at the front of the store that you think is causing your fear of being near that area?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think it’s that it doesn’t stay like a normal memory, if that makes sense. It’s not just, “Oh, there was a crash there and it scared me.” It’s more like the crash keeps forcing its way back into my head when I’m near that part of the store, especially if I’m trying to do something normal like set up a display.\n\nI’ll be arranging stuff or trying to focus on where things go, and then suddenly I can hear the bang again. Like, really clearly. And I see the glass coming down from the front windows, almost like it’s happening right in front of me again. It catches me off guard every time, because I’m not trying to think about it. I’m not standing there remembering on purpose.\n\nSo I think that’s the specific part that scares me — the bang and the glass. The front area kind of brings it back all at once, and for a second it feels like I’m back in it instead of just being at work.\n\nDoctor: Is there something specific about the crash, such as the sound or the broken glass, that you think is causing your fear of being near the front of the store?\n\nPatient: Yes… yeah, it’s definitely those parts. The sound and the glass. I know I keep coming back to that, but that’s what happens in my head. It’s like I can be doing something totally normal, like setting up a display near the front, trying to line things up or make it look nice, and then all of a sudden the crash just pops in.\n\nIt’s not like I decide to think about it. It just comes in out of nowhere, and suddenly I can hear that bang again, like really loud and sharp, and I see the glass coming down from the front windows. For a second it feels like it’s happening again, not like I’m just remembering it from before.\n\nSo, yes, I think the sound and the broken glass are the specific things that make being near the front so hard. The front area kind of sets it off, and then my mind goes right back to that moment.\n\nDoctor: Did the crash at the front of the store involve you directly or someone you know?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that. I mean, it was at the store where I work, so in that sense it involved me because I was there and it happened in the place I’m supposed to feel familiar with. But if you mean was I the one hit, or was there someone close to me personally involved… I don’t think I can really say that.\n\nWhat I do know is that it felt close enough that I can’t separate myself from it, if that makes sense. It wasn’t just something I heard about afterward. It happened right there at the front of the store, and that’s the part that stuck with me. But as far as whether it was directly me or someone I know specifically, I’m not sure there’s more I can give you than that.\n\nDoctor: Did you experience or witness the crash at the front of the store?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to separate those, honestly. Like… I was there at the store, and it happened at the front, so it wasn’t something I just heard about later from someone else. But when you say “experience or witness,” I don’t know exactly what counts.\n\nI don’t want to say it the wrong way or make it sound different than it was. I know I was there, and I know the front of the store is what’s tied to it for me. But whether I fully witnessed every part of the crash or was directly in it… I’m not sure how to explain that clearly. It all feels kind of jumbled when I try to put it into a straight answer."
